Newest at the bottom. Status: **Decided** (owner's call), **Accepted** (proposed, confirmed by owner), **Proposed** (awaiting confirmation). Reversing a decision adds a new row that supersedes the old one.

| D39 | 2026-10-11 | v0.0 implementation choices: (1) Preact pinned at 10.29.8, not 11.0.1 (11 was under two weeks old); TypeScript 5.9.3 per technical design §2. (2) The service worker is registered by hand in `platform/`, not via `workbox-window`, keeping runtime dependencies at two. (3) The numeric-literal lint is a small in-repo rule (`eslint-rules/`), scoped to `core/systems/` as technical design §5.1 says, because ESLint's `no-magic-numbers` allows any literal assigned to a `const`. (4) Core test files may use the non-portable `Math` functions, so T1.2's reference values can use `Math.pow`/`Math.log`. (5) Placeholder PWA icons and colors are drawn in-repo (`tools/make-icons.ts`) until the palette and art land (T1.26–T1.28). (6) The About screen shows an "Opened here" count per channel as the visible check that live and preview storage are separate. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D47 | 2026-10-12 | v0.1 section B choices (T1.9–T1.13): (1) The state adds a `time` field (simulation time, epoch ms) that technical design §6.2 relies on, and v0.1 `stats` of buildings bought and peasants settled. "Never settled" is stored as time 0 so saves stay valid JSON. (2) Settings are the four from GDD §9: theme, best-buy hint, rates per minute or hour, and reduce motion (system, on or off). (3) "×10" buys exactly ten or is refused; it never buys "up to ten". (4) No v0.1 command depends on the time, and an action is never refused because the device clock went backward (D21). (5) Best payback for housing places the new peasants one at a time into the most valuable open slots, after peasants who were already idle take theirs. So a Cottage with one free slot counts one peasant, and none if no slot is free. (6) Rates in breakdowns are per minute, as GDD §5.1.1 writes them. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D48 | 2026-10-12 | D47 (v0.1 section B choices) approved as proposed. | Owner | Decided |
| D49 | 2026-10-12 | v0.1 section C choices (T1.14–T1.16): (1) After a backward clock, catch-up credits no time and moves the game's clock back to the device's, so production carries on at once instead of pausing until real time catches up (D21: no penalty). The high-water mark stays, as technical design §6.4 says, so time already credited isn't credited twice. (2) A resource counts as having reached a cost within a relative tolerance of 1e-12, so an event fires at its exactly computed time despite rounding. (3) Catch-up is written so the core never waits: it hands back progress every 50 chunks, and the browser decides when to continue. (4) `advance` records when the game was last running (`lastSeen` and the high-water mark). | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D50 | 2026-10-12 | D49 (v0.1 section C choices) accepted as proposed. | Owner | Decided |
| D51 | 2026-10-12 | v0.1 section D choices (T1.17–T1.19): (1) The first save of each session copies the previous main save to the backup slot, if it loads cleanly; after that, every 10 minutes. (2) A save from a newer build is never overwritten: the game says so and doesn't start a new game over it. (3) A save that fails to load moves to `corrupt:<time>-<slot>`, and the next slot is tried (main, then backup, then a new game), with the reason reported. (4) Validation fills a missing ID with 0, repairs a broken job priority list, and resets an invalid setting to its default, each with a warning in the load report. Wrong types, negatives, fractional counts, unknown Ages, or more workers than peasants refuse the save. (5) Codes ignore spaces and line breaks from copy and paste; the checksum covers the code's payload text. (6) `rulesVersion` starts at 1. (7) The v1 fixture was made by playing a scripted three hours with the real game code. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D52 | 2026-10-12 | D51 (v0.1 section D choices) accepted as proposed. | Owner | Decided |
| D53 | 2026-10-12 | v0.1 UI choices (T1.20–T1.22): (1) The runtime keeps one live state with a version signal (technical design §10). It advances every second while the page is visible; after 60 s away it runs catch-up with a progress note, and after 5 min it shows the away sheet. (2) Brana and Pechko speak in the Hold header when something happens (first building, a peasant milestone, idle peasants appearing, the Age-up), cycling through their variants. Pechko's long-absence line shows on the away sheet. (3) After the Age-up, a sheet shows the restored gatehouse, the Roll of Honor and its inscription (v0.1 acceptance 1). (4) "Max" mode shows the cost of the most you can afford, or of one if you can't afford any. (5) Placeholder emoji stand in for resource icons until the art pipeline (T1.29). (6) The plain away sheet arrives with the shell; Reports (the last three) remain in T1.23. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D54 | 2026-10-12 | v0.1 UI choices (T1.23–T1.27): (1) Reports (the last three away sheets) and Diagnostics (the last 50 errors) live beside the save under their own namespaced keys, so they need no save migration and a bad save can't erase them. (2) Importing a code or file, or restoring the backup, asks for confirmation, then catches the save up from its own time to now (the offline cap applies). (3) "Reset everything" takes two confirmations and leaves the backup slot alone, so the old game can be restored until the next backup. (4) The debug time skip moves the game's timestamps back by the skip, then runs the normal catch-up to now, so the cap applies and the clocks stay aligned. (5) On desktop, hovering a number shows its breakdown in a popover. At 900 px wide the tabs become a left sidebar and sheets open on the right; at 1300 px the right panel stays open. (6) The install tip shows once, as a banner outside the installed app, and stays in More. (7) The debug panel's simulator button moves to T1.35, when the simulator exists. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D59 | 2026-10-12 | v0.1 art from the packs (T1.28, T1.31a, T1.20a): (1) Every UI color token is now a Zughy 32 color, in both the dark and light themes, and the app icon uses it too. (2) Building icons (32 px) and the two hold scenes (160 × 64, shown at 2×) are composed from Tiny Town tiles. | D60 | 2026-10-12 | v0.1 simulator choices (T1.34–T1.36): (1) Policies live in `src/core/policies/` (pure core code) rather than `sim/policies/`, so the debug panel's "run the Standard policy for N hours" is a `debug.runPolicy` command through `apply()` like every debug action. A check-in decision takes the state and the time to the check-in after next. (2) The simulated player's explicit rules, which GDD §5.1.2 leaves to the policy for items worth nothing right now: staff jobs the Age-up needs first, moving a worker over if nobody is idle; save for the first Mine once the first purchase is made; buy Silver Seams as soon as affordable; build the Age-up's required buildings; work on the bottleneck (a Cottage plus slots for the Age-up resource furthest from ready, silver aside, since Seams are capped); value a Cottage plus a slot building as one purchase when housing and slots are both full; turn auto-settle off while saving for the Age-up (GDD §5.1.1). Otherwise greedy by payback, as GDD §6.1 says. Without these rules pure payback never buys an early Mine (under the locked weights every job is worth the same per worker, so cheaper Farms always win) and stalls for good at 9 peasants. (3) A fix in the shared valuation, which the ● hint also uses: beds that already stand empty claim open job slots before a new Cottage does, so a Cottage no longer counts the same open slot again and again. (4) The virtual clock reads time as UTC; runs start Monday 2024-01-01 07:00 and Saturday 09:00. (5) Engagement checks look at check-ins from the start until the Keep. A check-in has "something to do" if it buys something, or passes up an affordable purchase to save for the Age-up; a wall is a run of check-ins without a purchase, at most 3, or 5 when it ends in the Age-up. (6) Each start time is judged on its own median (see Q4). (7) v0.1 has no randomness, so the 5 seeds give identical results; seeds start to matter in v0.2. (8) The overflow check scans every number in the state, timestamps included. (9) Until the v0.1 checks pass (T1.37, Q4), `npm run check` runs the fast set and writes its report without enforcing it; `npm run sim:check` and `sim.yml` enforce them and stay red meanwhile. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D61 | 2026-10-12 | v0.1 browser tests (T1.38): (1) `@playwright/test` 1.56.1 is added as a development-only dependency; it never ships in the game. (2) The smoke test runs on the real production build (`vite preview`) at a Pixel 7 phone size: buy a Cottage, reload, then reload offline once the service worker controls the page. A control run with service workers blocked fails offline, so the test really proves offline play. (3) The cross-engine determinism suite is one shared scenario (`tests/determinism/scenario.ts`): PRNG rolls, the portable math table, and a 3-day Standard game with a 13 h catch-up. Each browser's result must equal Node's byte for byte. Its page is served by the dev server only, so it never ships. (4) The smoke test runs in Chromium only, and the determinism suite in Chromium and WebKit, as the plan says. WebKit can't run in Claude Code's sessions (no browser there), so it is checked in CI, which installs both browsers. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D62 | 2026-10-12 | D60 and D61 approved. Q4 answered (owner took every recommendation): (a) "a fresh start under Standard" is the Opening run, 15 active minutes then Standard, so the Standard targets are measured on it; engagement checks skip the Opening's 10-second steps, as GDD §6.3 judges Standard check-ins. (b) Time targets are judged on the Monday 07:00 start; the Saturday 09:00 start is reported for times and judged by the engagement checks. (c) Tuning pass (T1.37): starting wood 50 → 75 (GDD §5.1.4 named 70; 70 gives 5.2 min, 75 gives 4.5 min) and the Keep's silver cost 300 → 250 (GDD §5.1.3). GDD updated to match. One refinement of D60's bottleneck rule, found in the tuning pass: food that auto-settle will spend on empty beds counts as food still needed; without it the policy kept 3 farmers and the Keep slipped to the next morning on food. Result on 5 seeds (Monday start): first Cottage 0 min, first Mine 4.5 min, Mill level 3 at 3 h, 10 peasants at 3 h, Keep at 14 h (Standard), 24 h (Light), 14 h (Heavy); every engagement check passes. Standard and Light land on the edges of their bands, because the Keep can only happen at a check-in. | Owner (Q4, D60, D61) + Claude (the bottleneck refinement) | Decided |

| D70 | 2026-10-13 | v0.2 combat engine (T2.5–T2.9): where the GDD is silent, the engine reads it this way (all exact-test-neutral; the §5.5.1 golden fight passes line for line). (1) Inside a monster action: start-of-action effects (Root-Wall, Lead Astray, Drowning Song, Enthrall, Riverhorse), then its attacks, then end-of-action effects (Noon Heat, Malice stacking). (2) "Every Nth action" counts the monster's own actions from 1: "1st, 5th, 9th…" (Drowning Song, Enthrall) is actions 1, 5, 9; "every 3rd" (Drag Under, Barrow-Grip) is 3, 6, 9. Barrow-Grip and Drag Under apply only if that attack hits. (3) Drowning Song replaces the attack (as written); Enthrall and Riverhorse happen in addition to the attack. (4) Riverhorse: the carried-off hunter misses the Nix's next 3 actions, returns before its 4th after the carry-off, loses 20% of max Health (can down them), and gains no meter while away; the fight only ends on "every hunter downed", not on one being away. (5) Drag Under's ×2 applies after Armor and taken%, before rounding. (6) Rounding: monster Health rounds half up when tier-scaled; monster Might and Armor stay exact; hunter max Health rounds half up; a Mender's heal is `round(1.5 × Might × (1 + Bonesetter))`. (7) Monster weapon attacks are Steel (it only matters for taken%); Noon Heat is Fire, ignores Armor, and takes taken%. (8) The Silver sub-hit draws no rolls of its own: it uses the main hit's variance and crit, and both are applied before death checks. (9) Shieldwall protects allies, not its holder. (10) Lead Astray picks the hunter with a second roll, uniformly among those present. The five seeded goldens in `tests/golden/` are recorded from this reading and are locked. Owner: say if any reading is wrong. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D72 | 2026-10-13 | v0.2 state and rules (T2.4, T2.10–T2.15): where the GDD is silent, the code reads it this way. **Saved game schema v2** adds hunters, roster, board, running and unread contracts, the Hall of the Fallen and a log of the last 50 fights; the migration gives the two free starting hunters, the new buildings at 0, and opens the board at once if a Mine already stands. (1) The first contract arrives the moment the first Mine is built, then every 2 h; when the board is full the timer waits, and a freed slot (decline or launch) restarts it at 2 h from then, so declining never rerolls instantly. (2) A contract's seed index counts every arrival ever, so ids never repeat. (3) Tier mix: 60% at the highest unlocked tier, 40% spread evenly over the lower ones; the village is picked evenly, then a place that village has (never barrow at tier 1), then a species that can be there. (4) A recruit joins the active roster if there is room, otherwise the reserve. (5) Hunters level up on their own as XP arrives (cost `50 × 1.5^(L−1)`), capped at level 10 in both Ranks (only the Keep cap exists in GDD §7.3; at the cap XP stays at 0). (6) Failures give half XP to every surviving hunter; a dead hunter gets none. (7) A launch computes the odds (500 fights) to know whether the contract is Reckless; Reckless needs `confirmReckless`, and the flag is stored with the running contract. (8) The Trophy Hall is a count building limited to one copy (a `maxCount` on the building, not a balance key). (9) A fallen hunter returns as a candidate 3 days after dying and costs their price again. (10) Unlock conditions: "Defeat an Upyr" and "Complete a river contract" count successes on those; "Earn 2,000 silver in bounties" counts bounty silver earned (lifetime). | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D73 | 2026-10-13 | Q8 answered: Suggest's stage 2 compares **3 finalists at 200 fights each**, and only the winner is rerun at 500 for the odds panel. This changes the locked `suggest.finalists` (5 → 3) and adds a locked `suggest.stage2Samples` = 200, with owner approval; GDD §6.5 and §7.4 updated in the same commit. Stage 1 (every legal party at 50 shared-seed fights) is unchanged. | Owner | Decided |
| D74 | 2026-10-13 | Suggest speed-ups that keep its answer (T2.18, with D73): (1) stage 1 hashes its 50 shared seeds once (`seedBank`, same streams as `seeded`), saving most of the seeding cost; (2) stage 1 checks full-size parties first, and only looks at smaller parties (cheaper provisions at the same odds) if some full-size party already wins all 50 fights; when none does, a smaller party would only add long fights and never wins the pick. Measured (best of 3, unthrottled): 3 free hunters 54 ms, 5 free about 170 ms, 8 free about 740 ms. Typical rosters (3–4 free hunters) fit the 300 ms budget at a 4× slowdown with room under the 2× fail line; 8 free hunters does not. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D75 | 2026-10-13 | The simulated player's contract rules (T2.25): it collects results; hires candidates cheapest first while leaving the next Rank-up's silver untouched; raises the Barracks when hunters wait in the reserve; upgrades gear weakest slot first within the Rank-up's reserve; and sends the highest-tier contract it can with the strongest party it has free, **never Reckless** (it only launches at 35% or better, judged on 60 fights) and only if provisions are affordable. Until the first contract of a game is sent, it holds back the food for its provisions and pauses settling. The odds-honesty check adds a probe that builds random parties and monsters across the whole odds range (strengthening each party until the odds land in a bucket that has room), so every 10-point bucket is judged, not just 90–100%. The probe plays 25 real fights per matchup, so a bucket's real rate isn't one coin flip. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D89 | 2026-10-13 | Investigation readings (plan T3.6) where GDD §5.6 is silent: (1) a contract counts as identified as soon as the clues so far leave one species, not only at the third clue, and its slot is freed then (the species-pair rule means two clues are usually enough, and sometimes one); (2) stopping a slot keeps the clues shown and loses the clue in progress; (3) a clue's time is fixed when it starts, so a Tracker leaving mid-clue does not change it; (4) each idle Tracker (owned, on the active roster, not out on a contract, not recovering) speeds up one slot, the first in the queue, and "25% faster" means the clue takes 25% less time (×0.75), stacking with Studied (×0.5); (5) a Mastered species makes a contract arrive identified only when the contract arrives, not for contracts already on the board; (6) when a contract arrives unidentified, every species it could be becomes Sighted; when it arrives identified, its species becomes Identified; (7) until the odds can average over candidates (T3.10), generation still posts every contract identified, so no odds leak. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D90 | 2026-10-13 | Knowledge (plan T3.7), readings where GDD §5.6.2 is silent. (1) Fighting a species Identifies it whether the fight is won or lost; only a win counts as a kill. (2) The knowledge level is frozen at launch, on the running contract and on the fight record (`knowledge`, 0–4), so a later level-up never changes a replayed fight; this adds one number to the v3 schema (it amends D88 before v3 ships; the migration sets it to 2 on the owner's old contracts and fights, which had no bonus). (3) The bonus (+5% Studied, +10% Mastered) applies to everything the hunters deal in a direct attack, shown in the fight log line; it does not apply to the Silver sub-hit (which has no percent bonuses at all) or to the monster. I will bump it if the owner prefers to treat the new inputs as a rules change. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D91 | 2026-10-13 | Brewing and the Garden (plan T3.8), readings where GDD §5.7 is silent: (1) a brew's recipe is paid when it is queued, so a queued brew can never stall for want of reagents; (2) cancelling any brew in the queue refunds its recipe in full; (3) the queue has no length limit (the recipe cost is the limit); (4) a Hut upgrade puts the next waiting brew on the new kettle at once; (5) the Herb Garden's clock starts when the first garden is built, each garden adds one of each herb per harvest, and a garden built mid-interval joins the next harvest; (6) a success's gift reagent is picked from the village's gifts by its own seeded stream (`${gameSeed}:reagents:${contractId}`), and a failure brings no reagents. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D92 | 2026-10-13 | Preparation in combat (plan T3.9), readings where GDD §5.7 and §8.3 are silent; each is in the code and tested. (1) **Items are spent at launch**, win or lose; one oil and one potion per hunter in the party, at most `prep.bombSlots` (2) bombs. (2) **Oil:** +35% in the hunter's outgoing% bucket (it adds to Grave-warden and the like, not on top); a mismatched oil does nothing. (3) **Potions fire on their own condition.** Quickfoot Tincture and Hardwood Tonic work from the start (the log shows the drink at tick 1); Mending Draught fires once, right after any damage that leaves the holder under 30% Health and alive. "Until the holder's 5th action" means the tonic covers the holder's first 5 actions. **Herbwise** multiplies the potion's numbers by 1.25 (heal 50%, Armor +37.5%, Speed +25%). (4) **Spoil** ruins a potion that has not fired yet, on the first hit on each hunter, which in practice means only an unused Mending Draught (the other two potions have already been drunk). (5) **Bombs** are thrown in order at tick 1, before anyone acts, ignore Armor, and use the species' resistance; they get no oil, trait or knowledge bonus (they are not hunter attacks). **Fire Pot** burn damage lands at the start of each of the monster's next 3 actions (before its attacks); a second Fire Pot refreshes the burn rather than stacking it, and likewise a second Frost Pot. **Frost Pot** slows for the monster's next 3 actions and also suppresses Noon Heat for 3 actions. (6) **Saltburst** goes off on the first moment one of the monster's abilities would take effect (a Hamstring that lands, a grip, a song, a Noon Heat tick, the Riverhorse, a Second Soul…): that effect is cancelled, and the monster's abilities do nothing for its next 3 actions (the action it goes off in does not count). Passive Swell cannot be cancelled. A Saltburst that never meets an ability trigger stays unused. (7) **Well prepared** needs the contract to succeed, a matching oil on someone in the party, and a bomb whose damage type the species is weak to (resistance at or above `prep.weakAt` = 1.25, the §8.3 bold rule) or a Saltburst that cancelled something; it pays bounty × 1.5 and +1 trophy. (8) The seed text is `<hunter>:oil:<item>`, `<hunter>:potion:<item>`, `bomb:<item>`, in a fixed order. (9) Two new balance keys, `prep.bombSlots` and `prep.weakAt` (both tunable), and a GDD §7.5 row documenting them; no design number changed. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D93 | 2026-10-13 | Odds and Suggest with uncertainty (plan T3.10), readings where GDD §5.6.3 and §6.5 are silent. (1) Contracts now arrive **unidentified** (no clues) unless the species is Mastered. (2) The odds panel averages over **every species the clues so far leave possible, equal weight**, each fought at its own knowledge level and with the same preparation (an oil only helps against the candidates it matches); the 500 fights are split equally, rounded up per candidate (so 501 for three), and the margin uses the total. (3) The panel and the launch **never depend on the true species**: the odds target is the same whichever species it really is. (4) The Reckless line and the stored win chance use the averaged odds the player was shown; the real fight and the death roll use the true species. (5) The Why tree for an unidentified monster has one child per candidate, named only if that species is Identified, otherwise "Candidate 1…". (6) Suggest's stage 1 uses 20 fights per candidate (stage1PerCandidate), cycling the candidates over the same seeds for every party; stages 2 and 3 are as in D73. (7) The odds summary ("Why") now includes the knowledge bonus and the oil. (8) Benchmarks at 4× slowdown, Chromium: odds 73 ms (budget 100), Suggest 253 ms (300), odds with 3 candidates and preparation 96 ms, Suggest with 3 candidates and preparation 281 ms: all under budget. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D94 | 2026-10-13 | Screens for the bestiary and preparation (plan T3.11), readings where GDD §9 is silent. (1) The **Bestiary tab** appears with the first species the player has seen (Sighted), and sits after Hunters. (2) An entry shows its blurb, "What it was" line, stats, resistances and ability wording from **Identified**; Studied and Mastered add their damage bonus; **Mastered** adds a counter-tip per ability (14 short tips I wrote, in `content/text/bestiary.ts`, each naming something the game really does; please read them). A Sighted species is a dashed "?" box, not a silhouette (the portraits have a solid backdrop). (3) The ability wording is built from the ability's numbers (`describeAbility`), not typed by hand. (4) A contract's clue panel says "A clue takes up to 30m" before it starts and the real time once it runs, so a Studied species is only given away once the investigation is under way. N candidates" and the candidates' names only where Identified; the launch sheet shows resistances and abilities only once the contract is identified; a running contract reads "Unknown monster" until it finishes. (7) Hut: opened from its building row; it shows the queue (with Cancel), stores, reagents, the garden's next harvest, and every recipe with Brew, muted with what is missing. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D95 | 2026-10-13 | The simulated player's v0.3 rules (plan T3.12; tunable behaviour of the simulator, no game rule changed). In the Keep it buys the Lodge, the Hut and a Garden in turn (then second levels, then a third Lodge level), as soon as it can afford each with its reserve left over. It keeps one brew per kettle going, oils first, up to a few of each. Against an unidentified contract it waits for the clues instead of sending the party blind unless its odds are already 90% or better; free Lodge slots go to the highest-tier unidentified contracts still on the board. At launch it carries, for a species it can name, the matching oil on each hunter, any bomb the species is weak to (a Saltburst if none), and a Mending Draught for the first hunter; against an unnamed monster, a Saltburst. It never goes Reckless, as before. New checks: **first-identified** (≤ 2 h after the Lodge; measured 30 min), **first-studied** (days 2–3; measured 14.5 h, marked pending Q16), **preparation-matters** (a probe over random parties and species: where the odds without preparation sit between 10% and 90%, matched preparation adds ≥ 15 points per tier; tiers 4 and 5 measure +40 and +46 points, tiers 1–3 have too few contested cases to judge). | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D132 | 2026-10-13 | T4.15, why Monday seed `sim-5` never finished cycle 2: the simulated player, not a game rule. In cycle 2 auto-settle sent every settler to farming (13 Mines, 1 miner), and the "shortest resource" rule always answered food, because it counted the food to settle every empty bed (about 20 million each at 90+ peasants) and ignored the Mill and Smithy levels Bastion requires, which cost iron. Also found: the player never saved silver for the Citadel (bounties, its main silver, show no rate, so "affordable within 2 check-ins" never came true and hires and gear took every coin; Citadel at about 126 h). Simulated-player changes (GDD §6.1), all from the Keep on (the Hamlet is unchanged, Keep still 14 h): (1) the shortest resource counts the cost of the buildings and levels the Rank-up still requires; (2) it counts the settling food only while the Rank-up still needs peasants; (3) auto-settle's priority puts the shortest resource's job first; (4) saving for the Rank-up starts once everything but silver is within 2 check-ins, and hires then leave the Rank-up's silver alone (amends D129 (2)). | D286 | 2026-10-15 | T7.8 readings where the GDD's blessing text leaves a detail open (all Proposed): (1) **One Thread:** every standing hunter other than the one healed, the Mender included, recovers 25% of what was actually healed. (3) **The Long Vigil:** every hunter at 0 Health gets up, including any downed on earlier floors of the run. (4) **Mended Cloth:** if the Mender is down at her next action, or is the hunter downed, nothing is lifted. (5) **A Price Paid:** the Health given is rounded down and never leaves less than 1. (6) **The Pack Grows:** a monster counts when it goes down for good (a Second Soul rising does not count until it falls again), and the 45% cap holds across floors. (7) **Toll and Struck Bronze:** Toll counts a hunter's first hit that lands on each monster; Struck Bronze applies to any hunter's crit on a monster still standing. (8) **The Wolf's Share:** the extra action costs no turn meter and does not repeat. (9) **Warp and Weft:** the Vanguard himself gets no bonus, and it applies while any other Vanguard stands. (10) A Delve fight carries each hunter's Health as a share of max Health, so a blessing that raises max Health raises current Health with it. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D287 | 2026-10-15 | T7.9/T7.10 readings where the GDD leaves a detail open (all Proposed): (1) **The Riftglass allowance clock** starts when a warden first reaches the Keep (Rank-up), so time spent in the Hamlet is not owed; the allowance grows continuously, a clock set back changes nothing, and only whole Riftglass is paid out. (2) **Late hours** is refused once the offline cap already sits at the 24 hour ceiling, so no purchase is ever wasted. (3) **Alternate traits** swap only the second trait; they are refused while the hunter is on a contract, in a Delve, or has not bought the trait; the simulated players never switch. (4) **Thrifty** cuts the provisions of any contract its holder joins by 15%; **Splint and Sling** speeds up the other hunters of the party, never its holder. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D288 | 2026-10-15 | T7.11 readings (all Proposed): (1) **The idle sources of the Delve relics are checked** when a contract fight raises a species' knowledge, when an experiment finishes, and when a Delve starts or ends, so a relic whose family was already Studied before Delves opened is found at the start of the first run. (2) **The Deep bestiary** is kept in the Delve state apart from the contract bestiary, so it can never count toward any bestiary total; the Act V true-ending count (not yet built) must read only the contract bestiary. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D289 | 2026-10-15 | T7.12 art readings where D265 and GDD §9.12 leave a detail open (all Proposed): (1) **A battle sprite is one PNG strip of five 32 px frames** (idle, a second idle that dips the upper body one pixel, lunge, hit flash, downed), all made from a single drawn base, so a redrawn base redraws every frame; hunters face right, monsters and guardians left. (2) **The hit flash** turns every body pixel white and keeps the outline; **the downed pose** is the figure turned onto its side in darker palette colors. (3) **Rarity frames** are 20 px borders (Faint plain grey, Warm gold with bright corners, Bright gold and ice with stars) that sit around a 16 px sigil or icon, and have their own asset kind. (4) **Hold cosmetics** are drawn as 160 × 64 transparent layers per Rank (16 images), so the screen only stacks them on the hold scene; their places were measured from the scenes and are the first thing to adjust after the owner looks. (5) The contact sheet now shows sprites frame by frame and the cosmetics on their scenes. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D290 | 2026-10-15 | T7.13a readings where GDD §9.15 leaves a detail open (all Proposed): (1) **The floor playback is worked out before the floor is fought**: the sheet replays the same fight from the run's seed with its log, and only when the playback has ended (or been skipped) does it ask the core to fight the floor, so a closed sheet leaves the floor unfought and the final floor (which ends the run) can still be shown. (2) **The guardian's one line** is its approved "What it was" line (lore §29). (3) **The Rift card** also shows how much Riftglass is held, and says *"The rift is quiet tonight"* instead of a number when nothing is waiting; the Hold tab shows a ● while a run is paused, a result is unread or the allowance is full. (4) **Away lines** ("A Delve is waiting on floor 3.", "Riftglass waiting: 40 of 140.") are built when the player comes back and stored in the away summary, so an old report never shows today's numbers. (5) **Reduced motion** (the Settings switch, or the device's setting when on "system") shows the fight's end at once in still frames, with the Log. (6) **Stopgaps until T7.13b:** a paused run on a door or blessing screen shows a plain note and Leave run (with a confirmation), and a finished run shows a plain result with a Done button, so no run can be stranded. (7) **The way-down scene** is shown once as a sheet; keeping it in the Library moves to T7.13b with the Echoes shelf. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D291 | 2026-10-15 | T7.13b readings where GDD §9.15 leaves a detail open (all Proposed): (1) **Doors choose on tap; a blessing is selected, then taken** with a button, so a stray tap never fixes a blessing for the whole run; the doors show the family ahead only ("Ahead: Cursed"), the floor view the species. (2) **The result sheet's "quiet tonight"** shows when the run earned no Riftglass and none is left; Pechko's line comes from the quiet set then, otherwise the guardian set (guardian beaten) or the early-end set (left or beaten), picked by the run's seed. (3) **Shop rows are disabled with the very sentence the command would refuse with** (the buy functions and the screen share one problem function each). (4) **Bought cosmetics are stacked on the hold scene** as the per-Rank layers of T7.12. (5) **The Library** gets an Echoes shelf (patron and rarity, the echo word for word) and a Scenes card keeping the way-down scene. (6) **Act IV:** a save re-based onto it meets Pechko on load, on a card that takes the Hold's place, like Arvid's; for a new warden the new-warden sheet says the approved arrival line, then D4-01 plays; scenes wait while such a card is due. (7) **The Last Stand's confirmation** adds *"Your Delve ends here; you keep what it earned."* while a run is paused. (8) **The Delve relics found** are listed at the foot of the Riftglass shop, since the Hearth lists only the hold's own relics. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D295 | 2026-10-15 | T7.15 readings where D271 and D272 leave a detail open (all Proposed): (1) **The Monday Standard and Delver runs keep playing past their 14-day minimum until each has all of "all content"** (up to 42 days), so the Idle-only row compares when each finished; the Saturday runs and the other policies are unchanged. | D296 | 2026-10-15 | T7.16 tuning (a `tunable` only): **`delve.baseTier` becomes keep 3 / citadel 4 / bastion 5** (was 2 / 3 / 4), so floors 1–3 are at tier 3 from the Keep, floors 4–6 at tier 4 and the guardian at tier 5. Why: every simulator row already passed, but the simulated Delver beat the guardian in 56 of 59 runs and cleared all six floors in every run (the owner's browser run said the same); no document gives a target, so this is a reading (Proposed, Q24): about 7 guardian wins in 10 runs for a player who picks the highest rarity and nothing cleverer, with floors 1–6 still almost always cleared. Measured with the same five Monday seeds: before 95%; this change 71% (47 of 66); this change plus memberHealth 0.75 and memberMight 0.8, 59%; two tiers up, 21% (and one seed never beats a guardian). Making the monsters themselves tougher without changing the tier did less (memberHealth and memberMight 1: 84%; with guardianHealth 3 and recovery 0.1 as well: 73%), and combined with one tier up it made a wall at the first three-monster floor (38% and 27% wins, most losses on floor 4). All 38 checks pass, the first guardian is still beaten in cycle 1 and Standard takes 0.84× the Delver's time to all content. GDD §5.10.3, §7.9 and the entry sheet's example line, and the tests that restate those numbers, are updated in the same commit. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D341 | 2026-10-15 | T8.10 readings (the yard, D315), where the docs leave a detail open: (1) **the amount is 25% of the XP one hunter on the contract earned, not 25% of each party member's added up** (D275 said "25% of the contract XP", and §7.10 says "share of a contract's XP"; the plan's "25% of each party member's XP" could also be read as a sum, which would give a bench hunter 75% of a participant's XP in a party of three) — the owner may want the other reading; it is one line to change; (2) it is paid for a win or a loss (a loss already pays half XP), and not at all when every hunter on the contract died, because nobody earned any XP to share; (3) a hunter who is away on a *different* contract still earns it, because D315 lists only the dead and hunters in a Delve as excluded; (4) the cap is read after the party's own XP is applied, is the hold's own level cap at most, and with fewer than three hunters is the lowest hunter's level (so a two-hunter hold catches nobody up); yard XP that reaches the cap is not banked beyond it, a hunter already at or above the cap is left exactly as they were, and hunters above the cap are untouched; (5) a yard level counts for the highest-level-ever record and the name letters like any level (the plan's Arvid, Sigrun and Toma catch-up); the result carries one `yard:<hunter>:<level>` line per hunter, with the level reached; (6) the Last Stand and Delves give no yard XP (contracts only, D315). | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D351 | 2026-10-15 | T8.15 readings (D320, D323): (1) the full suite is three shards by policy, played as parallel `sim.yml` jobs: `long` (Standard and the Delver, 15 runs), `rest` (Opening and Light, 20) and `heavy` (10), then a `judge` job that downloads the three result files and judges every row; the shard list lives in `sim/shards.ts`, the run list in `sim/specs.ts` (moved out of the command line tool, same runs, seeds and rows). (2) `npm run sim -- --check --shard <name>` plays one shard and keeps its raw runs; `npm run sim:check -- --combine <folder>` judges the runs in a folder, and refuses with a reason when a run is missing, repeated or foreign, so a lost shard can't pass as green. (3) Tested like D169: `sim/shards.test.ts`, and on the full suite the combined runs, checks and baseline equal a single-machine run exactly. (4) The 5-minute limit is enforced per job on the simulator step: a job over 300 s fails with a message to stop and ask (nothing is trimmed). (5) Workflow actions moved to the current majors (checkout v7, setup-node v7, upload-pages-artifact v5, deploy-pages v5, upload-artifact v7, download-artifact v8); the versions were read from each action's release page, and the first CI runs prove them. (6) `sim.yml` keeps its triggers (pull requests to `main`, on demand); this branch's runs are started by hand. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D352 | 2026-10-15 | T8.16 readings (simulated players' v0.8 rules, D321), where the docs say "fixed" but give no table; all live in `src/core/policies/depth.ts`, are the same for Standard, Light, Heavy and the Delver, and none counts as a "meaningful action" for the engagement checks (GDD §6.3 keeps its v0.1 meaning). (1) **Item score** = base Might + base Armor + 6 for each special effect (Silver or Rune sub-hit, Fire-taken cut) + each line's size × a weight (Might 150, max Health 100, crit chance 150, bane 60 per 1.0; flat Armor 2 and Speed 2 per point). Loot is equipped when it scores strictly higher than the slot's item (biggest gain first), for active hunters not away; what was worn goes to Stores. Stores above 30: the lowest scorer is salvaged first (ties: the older item). Reworking stays `gear.upgrade` as before. (2) **Ranks**: every hunter who qualifies, highest peak level first, before stars. (3) **Stars**: only from Embers that The Hearth's upgrades left over (they still go first, cheapest first); the cheapest star (the hunter with fewest stars), ties to the highest current level, then roster order. (4) **Talents**: lowest tier first, and within a tier the role's branches in this order: Vanguard Wall, Retort, Warden; Striker Edge, Hunt, Pace; Mender Hands, Watch, Herbs; a tier the hunter has too few stars for is skipped until they have them. (5) **Legendaries** (worn by the highest-level active hunter of the role with a free keepsake slot, in this order): Rune Nail and Coffin Silver and Wolf-Tooth String to Strikers; Barley Knot and Ferryman's Coin to Vanguards; Yrsa's Thole to Menders. A Legendary with nobody of its role free stays in Stores. (6) **The Delver's Re-roll** (D321, closes D294 (3)): once a run, on the first offer where every card is Faint, when the upgrade is bought; the new offer is then played as any other. (7) New report section "Hunter depth (reported only)": stars and Wrights as each cycle ends, Fine/Rare/Heroic items per cycle, Legendaries found and Delve XP. (8) Decisions run after the age-up and before contracts, so launches use the best gear. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

(2) **act-5** is judged like the other acts (each document on its own median over the Monday Standard seeds; D5-07 by cycle 6, the next warden). (3) **late-letters**: each of the five name letters (Ostap's two, Toma's, Sigrun's, Arvid's first) on its own median by the end of cycle 5. (4) **first-wright**: the median number of Wrights at the end of cycle 1 is at least 1. (5) The Monday Standard and Delver runs keep playing until all of the new "all content" is found, up to 42 days, so the five letters and every Wright are part of the stop rule. (6) The milestone becomes v0.8, so the three new rows are enforced; none is in the fast set (they need whole cycles), like the other story rows. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D354 | 2026-10-15 | T8.18 tuning, tunables only (three constants, GDD §5.3.2, §5.11.3, §5.14.4, §7.3, §7.7 and §7.10 updated with them): **`xp.growth` 1.5 → 1.3** (a level costs less as it rises: level 8 now takes 879 XP in all, was 1,609; the cap and the per-contract XP are unchanged), **`rank.wrightTrophies` 20 → 10**, **`ruin.ribbonshadeShare` 0.5 → 0.75**. Why: after the v0.8 players and rows the full suite failed `ribbonshades` (names found by cycle 6, median), `late-letters` (Ostap's level-8 letter at cycle 13: the main party levels too slowly for Standard to reach level 8 by cycle 5) and `idle-only` (Standard never finished "all content" within 42 days, mainly every hunter at Wright). **Result on the full suite (45 runs, one machine): all 41 rows pass.** Idle-only: Standard 387 h against the Delver's 384 h (1.008×); Ostap's level-8 letter in cycle 4; every hunter at Wright by about 290–360 h; Ribbonshade names by cycle 4; `trust-moves` 2 villages. **Tried and not taken:** `xp.perTier` 40 (doubling contract XP) passed the three rows but made `act-2` fail (D2-06 in cycle 5); `ruin.ribbonshadeShare` 0.9 (no better than 0.75 for the rows); `xp.perTier` 30 or `xp.growth` 1.4 (Ostap's level-8 letter at cycle 6–7). **Please look at:** this speeds the hunters' climb through the levels for every player (the cap, tiers and stats are as before), and two rows are close to their line (`act-2`'s D2-06 median is cycle 4 of 4; `ribbonshades` is cycle 4 of 4), as story rows always are with five seeds. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

| D358 | 2026-10-20 | **World-boss attempt, the shape.** (1) An attempt is **worked out at once from its seed and played back** with Skip and Log, like a Delve floor; nothing runs while the owner is away. (2) A party is **up to 4** hunters (`boss.partySize` 4, locked). (3) The fight ends when the party is down or at the 400-tick limit, like every fight (§5.5). (4) **All damage counts:** the boss enters the fight with Health equal to the pool still standing, and the pool after the attempt is the boss's Health when the fight ends. Healing a boss does in the fight comes off what the party dealt (the boss cannot end above the pool it started with). A boss at 0 Health is a kill. (5) **No injuries, no deaths, no recovery timers** from events; the 24 h rest is the whole cost (as with Delves and the Last Stand). (6) Preparation (one oil, one potion per hunter, up to 2 bombs) is spent at launch and works as for a contract; potions and bombs are spent whether or not the attempt goes well. | Owner | Proposed |
| D359 | 2026-10-20 | **Tokens, rest and time away.** (1) Tokens bank by **real time**, 1 per 8 h up to 6, shared by boss attempts and hunt attempts (one bank). (2) Rest is **per hunter** and blocks only other **event** attempts (boss or hunt) for 24 h; a resting hunter can still take contracts and Delves. Rest and banking never pause each other. (3) Both are derived from stored timestamps against `now`, never from `advance` events, so the offline cap does not apply to them: after 30 h away the owner has banked 3 tokens plus 6 h toward the next, and every rest that ended is over. (4) The bank is a **hard cap**: at 6 it shows *"Tokens: 6 of 6, full"*; time past full is simply lost, and the away sheet says so (it never says "nothing is lost"). The **full-bank badge shows only while events are open** (never while locked). (5) **Clock (D21, D49):** event timers run on a monotone `events.clock` that advances by the elapsed time **beyond the high-water mark**, so a clock set back and then forward again gives no free time (rewind, then a round trip, are both tested); remaining times are unchanged by a rewind. Riftglass differs on purpose (its allowance grows inside `advance` and respects the offline cap; tokens do not), and the sheet says nothing about that. (6) **The bank starts empty** at the moment events first open (reading, D369 (1)). (7) Tokens, rest and boss progress **survive the Long Night**. | Owner | Proposed |
| D360 | 2026-10-20 | **Bosses, order and pool.** (1) **One boss at a time, in a fixed order:** the Lindworm of Velk Fjord, the Barrow-King, the Mother of the River, then the cycle starts again one tier higher, so there is always a boss. (2) The pool is sized at **about 12 attempts** from what the simulated party actually deals; the starting sketch is `boss.poolBase` 24,000 Health at base tier 3, ×1.35 per tier above that (§7.4), so 32,400 at tier 4 and 43,740 at tier 5. The calibration task (T9.13) measures and returns the number as an owner-approved row. (3) The **visit tier** is fixed by the hold's Rank when the visit's **first attempt** is made (Citadel tier 4, Bastion tier 5) and does not change until that boss falls, so a Rank-up mid-visit changes nothing; the **second pass** through the three bosses adds one tier step, and past tier 5 the usual scaling continues. (4) After a **Long Night** the owner's Rank drops back to Hamlet: events are locked until the Citadel is reached again, but the pool, tokens and rests keep their state. | Owner | Proposed |
| D361 | 2026-10-20 | **Boss rewards and D315.** (1) Rewards use **existing systems only**: no new currency and no shop (GDD §4.1's "Boss marks" is dropped). Milestones fall at **25%, 50%, 75% of the pool and the kill**, each paid **once per visit**. (2) Each milestone pays silver (small, in same-tier bounties), reagents of the boss's kind, and the kill also pays **one item** rolled by the v0.8 loot rules (Stores cap, auto-salvage). **No Embers and no trophies** from bosses. (3) **D315 answered:** attendees earn **hunter XP** per attempt (reserves included); **yard XP stays contracts-only**; items come only from the kill. (4) Absence can delay a reward, never forfeit it (G7): milestones are paid by the attempt that crosses them, and nothing runs while away. | Owner | Proposed |
| D362 | 2026-10-20 | **Legendary Hunts.** (1) **Three hunts, one legendary hunter each**, each hunt with **three tracks**, each track restricting who can come (by role, trait or damage type): nine quarry fights in all. (2) **A hunt attempt is one fight** against the quarry on a chosen track, with a legal party of up to 4 (parties may be smaller). It costs **one token** (the shared bank) and puts every attendee on the shared 24 h rest. A win pays points, a loss pays fewer, so no attempt is wasted. (3) **Carry-over:** points stay across attempts, closing the app and the **Long Night**; nothing is in progress between attempts. (4) **On unlock** the legendary hunter **joins free** (active if there is room, otherwise the reserve) at the highest hunter's level capped by the Rank's level cap. **Rank rule (owner, D386):** they arrive as a **Wright only if that arrival level is 5 or more**; below 5 they arrive as an **Apprentice** and follow the normal rank rule. The jump this causes in the yard cap is accepted. They are hidden from the Hunters tab until unlocked. (5) An unlocked hunt stays open to **replay** for hunter XP and one reagent of the quarry's kind on a win; no points, no item. (6) Hunts are not injuries: no injuries, no deaths. | Owner | Proposed |

(1) A boss attempt's milestone is crossed when the visit's total damage (starting pool − pool left) reaches its share, and the kill when the pool is 0; an attempt pays every milestone it newly crosses, 25/50/75% at `boss.silverBounties.milestone` bounties and 3 reagents, the kill at `.kill` bounties and 6. (2) "One bounty" is the plain bounty at the visit's tier and the hold's age, without Haggler, Goodwill or preparation. (3) The kill's item uses the Delve guardian's bases (Silvered Blade or Emberwright Plate, half each) and `boss.itemRarityWeights`, from the stream `loot:boss:<boss>:<visit>`. (4) The attempt number is 1-based and part of the seed and of `contractId` (`event:<n>`); `FightRef.location` is null on event fights. (5) `EventsState.last` carries a full report of the latest boss attempt (damage, pool, milestones, silver, reagents, XP, level-ups, found keys, party, rest end), validated on load; it is still the v9 schema, since no v9 save exists outside this branch. (6) The Great Beast is Identified for the shelf by having a visit (`visit` > 0 or attempts made); no extra field. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D391 | 2026-10-20 | T9.7 readings (Claude's, for a yes or no). (1) **Boss Suggest's "damage floor"** (GDD §5.12.4, D387 (7)) had no number; the owner chose a new tunable, **`events.suggestFloor` = 0.08**: the share of the visit's starting pool that a party's typical attempt (the median of the shared stage-1 fights) must take off for the search to stop. (2) **`events.suggestTries` = 10 (tunable, new, for a yes or no):** Suggest also stops after 10 parties and takes the best seen, since the full search of 14 hunters (1,001 parties) took 9.5 s in Node. Both rows are in GDD §7.11; neither touches a `boss.*` value. (3) Parties are tried in a fixed order: highest level first, roster order second; full-size parties only; stage 1 uses `suggest.stage1Samples` shared dice (`eventsuggest` stream); only the winner gets the full 100-fight preview (no stage 2). (4) The preview's median, 10th and 90th percentiles are damage dealt, with `wouldPay` = the marks the typical attempt would newly bring. (5) **Proposed §6.5 rows (owner to set):** boss preview ≤ 150 ms and boss Suggest (14 hunters, 10 tries) ≤ 500 ms at the 4× slowdown; measured 52 ms and 375 ms (warn at the budget, fail at 2×). | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D392 | 2026-10-20 | T9.8 readings (Claude's, for a yes or no). (1) A hunt attempt is `hunt.attempt` with a track id; the points stop at `hunt.unlockPoints`, so the attempt that reaches it pays only the part that fits. (2) The legendary hunter's pieces are first-tier Common pieces made new for them with **no iron or silver recorded as paid**, so salvaging them returns nothing; they take an active place if there is room, else the reserve. (3) A track's restriction reads a hunter's **current traits** (an alternate trait switched on counts). (4) A loss pays `hunt.lossXpShare` of the win XP, rounded half up. (5) The hunt odds panel (`computeHuntOdds`) shows the win chance, its margin and the unprepared figure; it uses `huntodds` dice (party only, D98). (6) **Suggest for hunts and the worker message for the hunt odds are not built here**; they go with the hunt sheet (T9.14). (7) Hunt seeds are `${gameSeed}:hunt:${attempt}:${hunt}:${track}:${party}:${prep}` (technical design §7.2). | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D393 | 2026-10-20 | T9.9 readings (Claude's, for a yes or no). (1) **How the Dross-Tide is drawn:** a `dross` wave first draws its species exactly as a `base` wave does, then a second roll (its own stream, `${seed}:dross:${n}`) swaps in the Dross-Tide with chance 1 in (species that fit the tier + 1). So every wave it does not take is identical to the `base` wave, and the Dross-Tide is as likely as any one species (GDD §5.9.3: "differs only by the Dross-Tide"). (2) **"Act VI has begun"** is read as any act 6 or later in the save's act progress; the Act VI machinery itself is T9.10. (3) The pool is fixed on the cycle record at the Rank-up to Bastion (and by the debug jump), and also when a stand is made if still unset (the v0.4 arrival's stand can come at any Rank). (4) A stand record names its pool only when it is `dross` (`pool` on the record), so every saved stand reads and replays exactly as before and the replay needs nothing else. (5) The Dross-Tide fights as a guardian with no multiplier, no knowledge and no Rank bonus; it is named in the wave list at once because it can never be investigated. | Claude proposal | Proposed |
| D394 | 2026-10-20 | T9.10 readings (Claude's, for a yes or no). (2) **Start on load:** the v8 → v9 migration puts a story-less current cycle (one whose unwritten act number is above V) onto Act VI with fresh counters and clears the older cycles' act numbers above V, as v8 did for Act V; a save in Act V's cycle reaches Act VI with the next warden. An act whose opening scene is not in the game is never asked for on load. (3) **D6-10** uses a new field on the win-count condition (`withHunter`): the count and Sigrun on the roster, judged on a win and again whenever a hunter is recruited, so it waits and is never lost. (4) **Rime** is a document type with its own Library shelf (always shown, "Nothing here yet." when empty, pale text on a dark card); Rime documents show on the away sheet and results as the new-writing line, not as a letter with a bounty. (5) The arrival lines and the away-sheet line are OUT-3's words; the Accounts event "What the cold wants" exists with no accounts until the documents are in (T9.11). (6) The Rank-up sheet does not show Rank documents for any act today, so the Rime wording on it is not added here. | Claude proposal | Proposed |

(1) **Q31 (a), the owner's answer:** the Eventer alternates; after each boss attempt the next token goes to the not-yet-unlocked hunt with the most points (ties in list order), on its best-paying track with a full legal party, and never to an unlocked hunt; if no hunt has a full legal party it goes to the boss. This replaces the "within reach" test in GDD §6.1, which could never fire from 0 points (the best track pays 32, the total is 100). (2) It keeps spending while a token is banked and four fresh hunters remain, so one check-in can make several attempts. (3) It acts **before** the check-in's contract moves, since a rest from events never blocks a contract. (4) A role is required in the party only if the pool has it. (1) **Proposed: `boss.poolBase` 24,000 → 21,000.** With it the median attempts to kill a Great Beast across the three beasts, tier 4 (events just open, cycles 2 and 3) and tier 5 (first Bastion check-in), is 12.0 (quarter-to-three-quarter range 10.6 to 18.6). At 24,000 it is 13.7. If the first visit alone (the Lindworm) is the aim, 18,000 gives 12.1. (2) **Why one number is not enough:** hunter strength moves by a factor of two between moments (the Lindworm at tier 4 takes 6 to 21 attempts at 24,000), and the Barrow-King is far harder than the others in weak states (up to 112 attempts at tier 5 in cycle 3; the Mother needs 7 to 12). A table that evens them is in the calibration file; it changes GDD §8.13 (stats, resistances, one ability number and the notebook line "Steel does little"), so it is **not applied**. (3) **Hunts:** the tracks are cliff-edged (a best party wins 0% or 100%, seldom between); tracks I are at 100% everywhere, Likho's all 100%, Stoneback II and III 0% in weak states and 100% at the first Bastion. No quarry strength tried gave 25–90% in all states (tested up to 4× Health). With track III the unlock takes about 4 attempts (target 6–8). | D408 | 2026-10-21 | **What Orders are for (v0.10 flesh-out; the owner's answer to Q1).** Orders are **story first, with a choice that has a cost,** not a power tree. The cost is a **limited number of seats** (D412), so the player chooses whose story advances and whose small perk applies. Each Order gives a short questline (D414) and one small number plus one story or knowledge perk per level (D411). **No Order gives silver income, a fight number or a gate** (D418). The Orders are the four of lore §14: the Thanes' Moot, the Grey Wives, the Bell-Watchers and the Quenchers. The open questions in GDD §5.13 (exclusivity; whether reputation persists through the Long Night) are answered by D409 and D410. | v0.10 flesh-out, Q1 | Proposed |
| D409 | 2026-10-21 | **Orders are not exclusive in what you earn; they are limited in what is active (owner's answer to Q2).** (1) **All four Orders are always open to earn standing in** once each has opened (D413); standing is never lost and is not tied to a seat. (2) **Only a seated Order gives its perks and advances its questline.** An unseated Order keeps its standing and its due chapters, and they wait (D414); a waiting chapter never blocks another Order's. (3) **The Moot and the Quenchers are an opposed pair** (lore §14, §5): their **last chapters end differently** by whether the other is also seated (D414), and **each last chapter waits until the other Order has opened,** so no one is locked into an ending before the other side exists. (4) **Nothing required can come only from an Order** (lore §8): no required name, document, bestiary entry or Legendary is Order-only, and every Order's story is completable eventually by seating it. (5) The Grey Wives' hints and the Bell-Watchers' sharper text (D411) change *where to look*, never what exists. | v0.10 flesh-out, Q2 | Proposed |
| D410 | 2026-10-21 | **How standing is earned (owner's answers to Q3, Q6, Q11; refined at the review).** (1) Standing grows from **play already done,** with no new chores: **Moot** 1 per contract won, any village. **Grey Wives** 1 per brew, 3 per experiment, 10 per property revealed. **Bell-Watchers** 5 per document or hunter letter found, 10 per name found. **Quenchers** 2 per Kozly Hollow contract won; each Delve floor **whose result paid Riftglass** adds 1, capped per 7-day window on the event clock (`orders.delveWeeklyCap`). **Events add nothing in v0.10.** (2) **Each Triumphant stand adds a fixed chunk (`orders.triumphantChunk`) to every Order,** open or not. (3) **Four levels per Order** from thresholds in `balance.ts` (sketches). (4) **Standing never drops and is kept forever through the Long Night;** the highest level reached is saved so tuning can never lower a level. (5) **All four Orders count from v10 on, opened or not,** so nothing is reconstructed when an Order opens; **a save migrated from v9 gets one backfill at the migration, capped at level 1** (`orders.backfillCapLevel`), shown as a "brought from before the Orders" line, so a long-running save starts at the bottom and its first chapters come one at a time. What the save cannot recover starts at 0 and the migration names it (D421). (6) One Orders card shows level and count with a breakdown; **no new tab** (D416). (7) Every weight and threshold is a sketch the Orderer measures (D417). (8) Delve standing is a Delve reward with an idle source (Kozly Hollow contracts), so the Delve-optional test (D20, D271) stays true. | v0.10 flesh-out, Q3, Q6, Q11; review M2, M7, S8, S10 | Proposed |
| D411 | 2026-10-21 | **Perks (owner's answers to Q4 and Q10; refined at the review).** Each level gives **one small number and one story or knowledge perk, only while the Order is seated.** **Moot:** contract provisions food −3% a level (−12% at level 4); heads' lines in notices and a "What the heads say about you" entry on the Villages panel. **Grey Wives:** brew and experiment time −4% a level (−16%), **fixed when the brew or experiment is queued;** a hint toward a brew in the Book from "the eldest Grey Wife", **and an elder's note instead once the Book already holds that brew.** **Bell-Watchers:** clue time −4% a level (−16%); a sharper where-to-look sentence for still-missing names, **built by a stated rule from the roll,** and a remark by Halina or Dana once the group is found (OUT-3 E3). **Quenchers:** the hourly Threat rate −3% a level (−12%); a Kozly Hollow voice added to the Accounts event "What the hearth leaves" (OUT-3 E4). **There is no new bonus bucket (§5.0):** each number is its own named factor with its own breakdown line. **The Quenchers' perk is a trade:** a lower Threat makes each wave weaker (−0.61% Health and Might per point) and also lowers the Embers a stand pays (+0.7% per point of Threat); the Order sheet says so, and the stand preview uses the seated factor and recomputes at a seat change. The stand rows are re-judged on the Orderer (D417). Every step is a tunable named constant, bounded by the locked `orders.perkCeiling` = 20% (D418). | v0.10 flesh-out, Q4, Q10; review S3, S5, S9 | Proposed |
| D412 | 2026-10-21 | **Seats (owner's answers to Q5 and Q16; refined at the review).** (1) **Two seats at the start.** A **third opens only when two Orders are at level 4 and the Quenchers have opened** (both conditions, Q16 (a)); it is **checked after every level change, at the Quenchers' opening and on load,** so it opens whichever happens last; once open it is **kept forever through the Long Night.** The card reads, with the third seat closed: "Seats: 2 of 3 (the third opens when two Orders reach their top level and the Quenchers have come)." and, once open: "Seats: 3 of 3." and a second line "In seats: …" (or "In seats: none"). (2) **Each Order takes one seat; there is no partial seating.** (3) **Swaps are free at each new warden's arrival,** by a skippable **"Seat your Orders"** step on the new-warden sheet where the previous seats carry over by default. (4) **Mid-cycle changes** (seat, unseat or swap) start a **24 h cooldown on the monotone event clock** (`eventNow`, D359, D387), shown as "Can change in 9 h 20 m"; a rewound device clock gives no free change. A new warden's arrival clears it. (5) **Seating into a free seat at an Order's opening does not start the cooldown** (D422). **An opening scene's button reads "Seat it" only if a seat is free;** otherwise it reads "Continue". (6) **On load,** after the opening scenes, the same "Seat your Orders" step appears once as a sheet on the Hold screen (and stays reachable from the Orders card until used), free of cooldown. | v0.10 flesh-out, Q5, Q16; review M4, S6 | Proposed |

| D488 | 2026-10-21 | **Accessibility method rulings (red-team M7, S1–S6, N8).** (1) **Tabs at Largest:** the Archive section tabs **wrap to two rows** when they do not fit one row at the current Text size; the **bottom tab bar and the resource bar are exempt** from Text size (their labels are short and they must stay one row). "A screen cannot fit at Largest after this" is a stop-and-ask. (2) **Text size scales body-font text only.** The pixel font (headings and resource-bar numbers) and all sprites and icons **never scale** (integer scaling only, §9.12). Buttons are at least 44 × 44 px **at every Text size**. (3) **"Match phone"** means the app adds no scaling of its own. Whether the phone's own text-size setting reaches the page depends on the browser (Android Chrome follows it; iPhone home-screen apps generally do not); TV.4a checks what the build does today and the tests cover **only the in-app steps**. (4) **Tap targets:** WCAG 2.5.5 exceptions apply: inline text links in prose, the debug panel, and numbers that only respond to a long press; the ⓘ icons are **not** exempt (a 44 px hit area, visual size unchanged). (5) **The screen list** in OUT-4a is the single list used by the tap sweep, the Text size checks, the contrast checks and the layout checks. (6) **Reduced motion mechanism:** a script over the CSS allows `animation` and `transition` only under the reduced-motion gate; JS-driven motion goes through one registry; a Playwright `getAnimations()` check. Under reduced motion, fight and Delve playback and the reading keep the **same timeline and pace with still frames** (as D456), nothing fades, slides, bobs or flashes. (7) **Contrast:** pairs are derived in Playwright from computed `color` and `background-color` on the screen list in both themes; scope is **text and UI glyph icons** (multi-colour pixel art and text drawn over the pixel hold scene are looked at in the playtest, not measured); fixes use **existing theme tokens**. (8) **The styled cards** (the Rime reader's pale-on-dark, the document reader's parchment card and Ilse's italic note, the reading screen's dark card, scene sheets) **meet the same ratios in both themes;** a fix may brighten them, and if it would visibly change an approved look, show before/after and ask. | Claude proposal (red-team review) | Proposed |
